# Exit-buffer bakeoff — S&P 500

Generated 2026-09-05T03:07:44.267Z in 4.9s by `scripts/buffer-bakeoff-sp500.mjs`.

## Run

```bash
node scripts/buffer-bakeoff-sp500.mjs
```

Prerequisites: network to fetch the S&P 500 list, MySQL with `backend/.env`,
`daily_stock_data` populated (`npm run get-bulk-price-data`), and
`symbol_daily_scan` populated (`npm run analyze-symbols`) for optimized MA periods.

| Setting | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Universe | S&P 500 constituents (https://yfiua.github.io/index-constituents/constituents-sp500.json) |
| History window | `HISTORY_YEARS` = 3 |
| Bar dates observed | 2023-09-05 → 2026-09-02 |
| Symbols requested | 503 |
| Symbols analyzed | 496 |
| Symbols skipped | 7 |
| MA type | SMA |
| Open confirm | single (one close above fast) |
| MA periods | per-symbol from `symbol_daily_scan`, else 21/50 |
| Buffers tested | 0, 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 3, 4, 5 |

Only `exitBufferPct` varies. Every other open/close rule is unchanged, and signals come from
`simulateTrades` in `frontend/src/tradeSignals.js` — the same engine the Chart tab and nightly
scan use, so buffer 0 matches the Chart with Exit buffer % set to none.

Symbols with no closed trades at a buffer have no defined return or win rate; those cells are
excluded from the medians and means (counted in the last column) so that a buffer which merely
suppresses trading cannot look good on a smaller, luckier sample.

## Conclusion

**No buffer beat 0 on median return.** Baseline median is +25.1%; the best non-zero buffer is 0.5% at +21.5%. On this sample the exact fast-MA exit holds up.

No non-zero buffer improved more symbols than it worsened.

Median is the headline number because a single outlier symbol can dominate the mean; compare the two columns before acting on either.
